docs/adr/2026-08-28-fixed-vs-recurring-events.md:30:# Fixed vs Recurring events: un-conflating anchor_activities
docs/adr/2026-08-28-fixed-vs-recurring-events.md:32:> **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-26 by [`docs/adr/2026-09-26-fixed-recurring-event-identity-model.md`](2026-09-26-fixed-recurring-event-identity-model.md) (accepted).** This ADR proposed **un-conflating `anchor_activities` into two scope-keyed ENTITIES** (Fixed = all-camp, Recurring = group-scoped). T267 decided the opposite and shipped it (PR #560, schema v77): **one table** — `anchor_activities` renamed to `fixed_events`, with the fixed/recurring distinction kept as the existing `kind` column, not as a table split. T267 also gives the row a real `activity_id` (the identity fix this ADR did not address). The entity-split proposed here was considered and **rejected** as disproportionate; recorded so a reader does not build on it. This file is retained for that rationale, not as current instruction.

docs/adr/2026-09-12-activities-as-one-entity-with-placement.md:16:> **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-26 by [`docs/adr/2026-09-26-fixed-recurring-event-identity-model.md`](2026-09-26-fixed-recurring-event-identity-model.md) (accepted).** This ADR proposed keeping the name `anchor_activities` and treating fixed/recurring/general as a **classification COMPUTED at render time from coverage**. T267 decided and shipped the opposite (PR #560, schema v77): the table is **renamed to `fixed_events`** and the distinction stays a **STORED `kind` column** (derived-at-render kind was explicitly rejected as contradicting the shipped `kind` CHECK constraint). The one durable idea here — giving the placement row a real `activity_id` foreign key — was **carried forward** by T267; the computed-classification and keep-the-name proposals were **rejected**. Retained for that rationale, not as current instruction.

docs/adr/2026-09-12-activities-as-one-entity-with-placement.md:77:| **A** | One `activities` table; placement as a `placement` enum + nullable pinning columns. `anchor_activities` retired. | **Rejected.** Nullable-column soup on the busiest table; the `kind` CHECK invariant degrades into internal consistency nobody enforces. Forces an Automerge entity-count change (28 → 27) and a history-translation problem. Highest cost, lowest reversibility. |
docs/adr/2026-09-12-activities-as-one-entity-with-placement.md:78:| **B** | `activities` = catalogue; `anchor_activities` = placement child record with a real FK. Classification computed from the row's coverage. | **Chosen.** Strongest fit to the owner's model, expresses `Lunch 4` with no special case, additive and backward-compatible to Automerge, and is the version that does not need undoing if "one placement per Activity" is ever revisited (drop a uniqueness assumption → 1:N). |

docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:45:### Verified: the "engine doesn't see elective/anchor/event locations" premise is now false
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:47:An earlier note (`project_unified_schedule_overlay_model` memory, Slice 4) recorded that anchor/event/elective cells in the engine never registered their location in the capacity map. **That gap has since been closed.** `src/engine/buildSchedule.js:212-218` defines `registerOverlayOccupancy(locId, ...)`, which writes into the same `placeUsage` map that `placeBlocked` reads, and it is called for anchors (`:238`), events (`:251`), and elective offerings (`:280`). Anchor/event location columns exist (`anchor_activities.location_id`, `events.location_id`, schema v45) with a UI to set them (`LocationPicker`, merged). The engine's generation-time capacity check is therefore already correct across all four occupant kinds (regular activities, anchors, events, electives) — the problem is exclusively that this correctness is a single point-in-time fact that nothing re-checks afterward.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:57:  if (s.is_anchor || !s.activity_id) continue
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:63:### Anchor semantics (shipped, same date, in the branch that also fixed elective occupancy)
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:65:A companion fix, landed in the same branch as this ADR's filing, settled how `computeOverlaps` treats anchors — a question this ADR's original text left implicit by grouping anchors with "overlay occupants" generally (see the now-corrected `event_id`/anchor phrasing in the Decision section below). The shipped rule, **not** "count the anchor as an ordinary occupant":
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:67:- An anchor is immovable declared truth about where a group is, and is **never itself flagged**.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:68:- A place-capacity or activity-cap bucket made **entirely of anchors** (the canonical case: every group's Flagpole anchor, all at the same capacity-1 Flagpole location, every day) is **never flagged**, at any count — this is correct by construction, not an overbooking, and naive occupant-counting would wallpaper every Flagpole cell in every imported camp.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:69:- A **mixed** bucket (an anchor plus a non-anchor placement) counts the anchor toward the capacity total (an anchor already filling a capacity-1 place makes any additional non-anchor booking over capacity) but flags **only the non-anchor rows** — the anchor can't move, the placement can.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:70:- Anchor-vs-anchor is never a conflict.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:72:This does not change any recommendation below, but any future extension of `computeOverlaps` (including whatever comes out of this ADR) must preserve this rule rather than reverting to the simpler "anchors are just another occupant" model the Decision section's `event_id`/anchor phrasing could be read to imply.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:86:1. **`computeOverlaps` (`src/utils/computeOverlaps.js`) gains occupant-kind awareness.** ~~Today it buckets rows by `(day, block, location_id)` derived from `activity_id → actMap → location_id`, skipping anything without an `activity_id`. It must instead resolve a location for any occupied slot kind — `activity_id`, `elective_set_id`~~ **Corrected: `activity_id` and `elective_set_id` occupancy are both already handled — see the correction note in Context above.** Anchor occupancy (via `is_anchor`) is also now handled, with the anchor-specific semantics described in that same section (never flag an all-anchor bucket; flag only the non-anchor rows of a mixed bucket) — **not** by treating an anchor as an ordinary occupant. ~~and `event_id`/anchor occupancy the same way `registerOverlayOccupancy` does~~ — **`event_id` occupancy is a deliberate non-goal, not a remaining gap; see the Non-goal below.** The activity-cap bucket (`max_groups_per_slot`) mirrors the same anchor treatment (all-anchor bucket never flagged, mixed bucket flags only non-anchor rows) and stays activity-only otherwise, since instructor/equipment caps are a property of the activity, not of overlay occupants.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:88:**Non-goal: `event_id` occupancy is explicitly excluded from contention, by product decision, not by oversight or future work.** The product owner has ruled that events are deliberately outside the normal course of the schedule ("they are not supposed to be tied to the schedule because they are outside the normal course of events") and must not register as location occupants for `OVERLAP` purposes. This ADR's Decision, Files/modules affected, and Open Questions sections below were originally written recommending `event_id` be folded into the same occupant-kind-aware bucketing as `elective_set_id` and anchors; that recommendation is withdrawn. Any future work must not add event occupancy to `computeOverlaps` without a new product decision superseding this one.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:117:- (c) **Derive contention on Generated only for overlay-caused clashes** (electives/events/anchors), since those are exactly the things that change independently of the schedule itself, while activity-vs-activity contention stays engine-guaranteed. A genuine option, rejected below.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:137:- **`src/utils/computeOverlaps.js`** — ~~extend the place-capacity bucketing loop to resolve a location for `elective_set_id` and `event_id`/anchor occupants (not just `activity_id`)~~ **corrected: `elective_set_id` occupancy and anchor occupancy (with anchor-specific never-flag/mixed-bucket semantics, not naive counting) are both already shipped — see corrections in Context above. `event_id` occupancy is an explicit non-goal, not remaining work.** Function stays pure, stays render-time, stays unpersisted.
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:144:**Reused:** `computeOverlaps`/`withOverlapFlags` (existing, extended not replaced), the `OVERLAP` flag kind and its Findings Rail plumbing (existing), the overlay-occupant-to-location derivation pattern (`registerOverlayOccupancy`, existing in the engine, applied to a second consumer), `locations.capacity` and `activities.location_id`/`elective_set_activities`/`anchor_activities.location_id`/`events.location_id` (all existing schema, no migration).
docs/adr/2026-09-05-generated-route-render-time-location-contention.md:157:4. **Should the write-time `locationFull` check in `useSlotMutations.js` also become overlay-aware** (currently it only checks `activity_id`-keyed co-occupants — `computeOverlaps` has since closed this gap for elective and anchor occupancy, per the corrections above, but `useSlotMutations.js`'s write-time check has not been revisited), so that a drag onto an elective-occupied room is caught at write time too, not just at next render? **Events remain out of scope here too, per the Non-goal above.** This is a smaller, closely related fix, worth deciding whether it ships alongside this ADR or as a fast-follow — flagging rather than deciding, since it is a scope call, not an architecture call.

docs/archive/completed-plans/2026-05-30-anchor-spans-overlays.md:9:**Goal:** Add multi-block anchor spans (with unit scope) and a post-generation field trip overlay stamp tool to the scheduling app.
docs/archive/completed-plans/2026-05-30-anchor-spans-overlays.md:11:**Architecture:** Three coordinated changes: (1) the engine gains anchor `span_blocks` and `unit_id` scope so one anchor definition covers an entire unit and/or multiple consecutive blocks; (2) `ScheduleScreen` gains an overlay system backed by a new `template_overlays` DB table for field trip stamps that sit on top of the generated schedule; (3) a pull drawer UI lets users stamp overlay cells onto the schedule and extend them vertically via a fill handle drag.

docs/archive/completed-specs/2026-07-21-phase2-authorization-layer-design.md:11:Today, `users.role` (`'admin'|'staff'`) is the only role concept, and it is enforced in exactly one place server-side: `createUserHandler` in `electron/main.js` (confirmed admin-gated). Every other privileged-feeling action — camp/tier/group/activity/timeblock/anchor/cohort/dayoverride writes, schedule generation, conflict resolution — has no confirmed server-side role check. docs/archive/legacy-architecture/2026-07-21-renderer-migration-a-foundation.md:19:**Success predicate:** new `list(entity)` in `preload.js` → new IPC handler in `main.js` that validates `entity` against a fixed allowlist (mirroring how `electron/ops/projections.js`'s `PROJECTIONS` registry already validates writable entities) before querying. For the 9 camp-scoped-directly tables (`groups, tiers, activities, template_slots, cohorts, days_of_operation, time_blocks, anchor_activities, schedule_templates, day_override_templates`) returns `SELECT * FROM <table> WHERE camp_id = ?` (using the single camp row's id). **For the 3 parent-scoped tables (`template_overlays`, `schedule_snapshots`, `day_override_template_slots`) do NOT filter by a literal `camp_id` column — it doesn't exist on these tables. Scope them via JOIN through their parent (`schedule_templates`/`day_override_templates`) to the camp, e.g. `SELECT t.* FROM template_overlays t JOIN schedule_templates st ON st.id = t.template_id WHERE st.camp_id = ?`.** This was flagged by Red Hat/Grader on Task 1's review round as a real cross-task contract note, not speculative — build it in from the start rather than discovering it via a Task 2 test failure. Malformed/unrecognized `entity` values are rejected, not silently used to build a query.

docs/work/tickets/T301-linked-elective-choices-never-reach-the-solver.md:232:  emits one row per member each repeating the cluster's `Count`, showing only the anchor day even
docs/archive/completed-specs/2026-07-25-camp-id-projection-guard-design.md:20:Every projection whose `fields` array includes `'camp_id'` (`users`, `cohorts`, `groups`, `days_of_operation`, `time_blocks`, `tiers`, `activities`, `anchor_activities`, `day_override_templates` — 9 of the 12 entries in `PROJECTIONS`) will happily run `UPDATE <table> SET camp_id = ? WHERE id = ?` for **any** value, from any op. A malformed or malicious op with `field: 'camp_id'` silently repoints an arbitrary row to an arbitrary camp id.
docs/archive/completed-specs/2026-07-25-camp-id-projection-guard-design.md:44:Placed after the `!projection.fields.includes(op.field)` check (so unknown fields still short-circuit first, unchanged) and before `ensureExists?.()` runs, so a rejected `camp_id` write never reaches `ensureExists` either — several `ensureExists` implementations (`cohorts`, `groups`, `days_of_operation`, `time_blocks`, `tiers`, `activities`, `anchor_activities`, `day_override_templates`) insert a placeholder row keyed off whatever camp they look up themselves (always `SELECT ... FROM camps LIMIT 1`, never `op.value`), so they were never the vector for this bug — but ordering the guard first is still correct because a legitimate first-time `camp_id` write (row creation) must equal the device's own camp id anyway, and rejecting early avoids doing any DB write work for an op we're about to discard.

docs/work/tickets/T117-imported-schedule-as-version-slice2.md:49:2. **Anchor-first**: look up `activityName` in `anchorIdByName`. If found: `{ ..., anchor_id: id,
docs/work/tickets/T117-imported-schedule-as-version-slice2.md:50:   activity_id: null, is_anchor: true, flags: {} }`.

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:33:_Prior: 2026-09-29 (**A director can now change, add or withdraw one camper's elective preference from inside the app and solve the run again, without re-importing a file — T297, no schema change.** The affordance is on the per-camper week `src/screens/elective/run/CamperWeekPanel.jsx` (T296's view), because that is the screen where a wrong answer is noticed; the writes live in `electron/ops/setElectivePreference.js` behind the same admin-only `elective_assignment_runs.write` action the placement move uses, with `describeWriteFailure` on every call in `DraftRunView`. THE SCOPE OF AN EDIT IS INHERITED FROM THE ROW IT CORRECTS, which is the load-bearing detail: the engine's `rankAt` prefers an occurrence-scoped rank over a whole-run fallback for the same choice but simply TIES two different choices at equal rank, so correcting a whole-run ranked list with a cell-scoped row would leave the old answer live and the correction might lose. PROVENANCE: every edit op carries `source:'human'` and a withdrawal is an explicit `source:'human'` delete, so `isHumanOwned` (`electron/ops/fieldProvenance.js`, ADR 2026-09-09) distinguishes a correction from an import — and `commitElectiveRun` now stamps its preference writes `source:'import'`, without which every imported row read back as hand-edited and the marker meant nothing. A re-import PRESERVES a correction and REPORTS it as a `PREFERENCE_EDIT_HELD` finding, one per held row; it never overwrites one and never preserves one silently. Two gates are needed because a changed choice writes a new derived id: a field-level `isHumanOwned` check, and a tombstone check following `ingest.js`'s `rejectedSlotKeys`, which is the one that stops a re-import re-creating the superseded row. A re-solve now reads the run's own preference rows — `getElectiveRun` returns them alongside the run's `choices`, and `AssignmentPanel`'s solve passes BOTH to `buildElectiveAssignments`. The choices are what make this work: the engine resolves a stored row's `choice_id` only when it is also handed the run's choices, and nothing passed them before, so an unresolved row ranks nothing at all and the solve fills the week with fallbacks while reporting no problem. The MCP surface gained `camper_preferences`, `set_camper_preference` and `remove_camper_preference`, the read carrying `edited_by_hand` so an agent cannot overwrite a director's correction unknowingly. TWO CONSEQUENCES OF THE IMPORT STAMP WERE FIXED WITH IT: `electron/ops/attributeElectiveSubject.js` re-derives every preference id when it names a subject, so it now carries each row's provenance across that rekey — otherwise naming one camper silently relabelled their whole imported sheet as the director's own corrections, and the next import would hold every row and claim they had edited it. And the dev mock now derives `choice_id` onto its assignment rows as the real commit does, without which the camper-week panel found no preference to correct and offered an ADD where the real path performs a REPLACE. ~~KNOWN LIMIT: a run opened cold from the run list can be edited but not re-solved, because `onRegenerate` needs template occurrences this session has not derived — T250's pre-existing gap~~ CLOSED by T250 slice 5b (#667) — a cold-opened run now hydrates the state `onRegenerate` needs and re-solves onto its own runId. ~~So T297 stays open.~~ T297's own status is unchanged and was not re-evaluated by this sweep.) **Every finding on the elective assignment preview now names an activity the way the DIRECTOR spells it, not by the internal normalized label key — T300, no schema change.** `electiveChoiceLabelKey` (`electron/ops/electiveDerivedIds.js`) is `whitespaceInsensitiveName`: it lowercases and DELETES whitespace, so two devices agree that `Archery` and `archery` are one elective. It is an IDENTITY key and never display text, but it was the only spelling a finding producer held, so four findings interpolated it into sentences a director reads — `BELOW_MINIMUM` and `KEPT_BELOW_MINIMUM` (T265) and `UNSUPPORTED_LINKED_CHOICE` (T247) from `src/engine/buildElectiveAssignments.js`, and `UNRANKED_OFFERING` from `findMismatches` in `src/screens/elective/assignment/buildOfferings.js`. A name with a SPACE is worse than the lowercase: `Arts & Crafts` reached the screen as `arts&crafts`. The fix is at the RENDER, not in the producers: the engine is a pure function with no access to display names and `findMismatches` is the same shape one layer out, while every one of these findings already carries the identifier a name can be looked up FROM and all four converge on ONE rendered list (`src/screens/elective/assignment/AssignmentPreview.jsx`). `src/screens/elective/assignment/findingDisplayMessage.js` resolves the name there — by `activity_id`, else by matching `electiveChoiceLabelKey(activity.name)` against the finding's `labelKey`, else by `choice_ids` against an optional `choices` prop — and every producer is byte-unchanged. THE COUPLING THIS BUYS, stated because it is the thing that can rot: a producer hands the renderer a finished sentence with the key already inside it, so the renderer anchors on the QUOTES producers wrap labels in (curly in the engine, straight in `findMismatches`) rather than replacing the bare key — an elective named `Run` would otherwise turn “campers it needs to run” into “campers it needs to Run”. If a producer stops quoting, the substitution silently stops, which is why the render tests call the REAL producers instead of hand-writing findings. A finding the renderer cannot resolve is passed through VERBATIM — a sentence with a key in it still tells a director what happened. `UNSUPPORTED_LINKED_CHOICE` is unreachable from this screen today (`AssignmentPanel.jsx` passes no `choices`/`choiceOfferings` to the solver, so the linked tier never runs); the renderer is wired for it and the gap is recorded rather than faked. Also in this slice by owner ruling 2026-09-29: the same summary line read “1 campers placed · 1 occurrences · 1 findings” and now pluralizes all three counts, as the per-occurrence header one component down already did. Prior header note follows.)_

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:92:_Prior: 2026-09-24 (**A peer's same-named record is no longer silently discarded — ten name-UNIQUE constraints relaxed, four kept and made loud (schema v73, T241/T235/T238/T252/T242/T239).** `detectUniqueFieldCollision` was never invoked from the Automerge merge-apply path, so a UNIQUE-colliding row arriving from another device was caught by `projector.js`'s `upsertRow` SAVEPOINT, written to `projection_failures`, `console.error`'d, and **lost with no director signal**. `UNIQUE(camp_id, name)` on a director-typed name was not enforcing an invariant a replicated document can hold; it was discarding data to preserve one. **Relaxed (ten):** `locations`, `activities`, `events`, `elective_sets`, `groups`, `cohorts`, `tiers`, `time_blocks`, `schedule_weeks`, `special_days` — both rows now project, and a duplicate is **derived at render time** (`src/screens/duplicateSiblings.js`, `normalizeWordKey`, exact matching) as a per-row marker via the new shared `src/components/setup/DuplicateNameDot.jsx` on Groups, Cohorts, Tiers, Time Blocks, Electives and Special Events; Locations and Activities already had one. Nothing about the duplicate is stored, broadcast, or resolved by the app — it clears when the document stops holding two such rows. **Kept hard (four):** `users(camp_id,name)` (`localAuth.js` resolves a login by name — a duplicate would authenticate a director against an arbitrary one of two PIN/role pairs), `days_of_operation(camp_id,day_of_week)`, `schedule_templates(camp_id,kind)`, `camp_maps(camp_id,kind)`. Their collisions are now derived from the merged document by the new pure `electron/automerge/uniqueConflicts.js` (`deriveUniqueConflicts`) — a **second** guard hung off the same `assertConflictsRecorded` choke point rather than a widened `reconcile()`, so both kinds are unbypassable without merging their shapes — recorded under a `unique:` id namespace into `conflicts` (which gained `entity_ids` and `kind`), and rendered by `ConflictsScreen`'s buttonless informational `UniqueConflictCard`. **The migration is a TABLE REBUILD for nine of the ten**, because an inline `UNIQUE(...)` in `CREATE TABLE` compiles to a `sqlite_autoindex_*` that `DROP INDEX` cannot touch — had this shipped as a plain index swap, a migrated db would have relaxed while a **fresh install kept the constraint**, behind a green gate. `v73_down` refuses across all ten tables at once, naming every offending value and row id, and never dedups silently. **Three defects were found by the tests rather than in production:** `PRAGMA foreign_keys=OFF` is a no-op inside `db.transaction()`; `INSERT INTO ... SELECT *` is positional, not by-name, and misplaced a column on a real ALTER-accumulated path; and a `unique:` conflict on `users` carries two WHOLE user records, so the pre-existing IPC sanitization — written for the scalar `value` path — passed `pin_hash`/`pin_salt` straight to the renderer until `sanitizeRecordForIpc` was added. **Known and deliberately open:** `schedule_weeks` is relaxed in the schema but has **no duplicate marker**, because weeks are authored inside `ScheduleScreen` and no setup screen owns them; and three of the four hard-set kinds (`users`, `camp_maps`, `schedule_templates`) have **no director-facing screen to resolve on**, so their cards name the collision honestly without pointing anywhere — only `days_of_operation` gets a "fix it on the Days screen" line. **Follow-on, T255 slice A:** relaxing the constraint also made a dozen name-keyed *lookups* reachable in their most dangerous form — `new Map(rows.map(...))` is last-write-wins, so the lookup SUCCEEDS against an arbitrary same-named row and the director sees a clean import with an elective bound to the wrong period. Four ingest seams now refuse instead of guessing, via the new shared `src/ingest/mapWithCollisions.js`: a colliding key is DELETED from the returned map, so a caller that ignores the `ambiguous` set still cannot bind to a wrong row. The refused name rides the ambiguity channel each site already had (`unmapped` in `electiveSetPopulate.js` and `eventGridPopulate.js`; `activityId: null` plus `ambiguous_activities` in `specialDayPlan.js`'s `blockedBy`, now rendered by `ImportScreen` and guarded so a future `blockedBy` reason cannot ship without an explanation). Deliberately NOT a lowest-id tie-break: these four run in front of a director about to click confirm, so refusing beats guessing deterministically — the tie-break is right only where no human is in the loop, which is where T252 applied it. **Slice B** extends the same refusal to the three XLSX import screens and elective attendance: `ActivitiesScreen` (`tierMap`/`actMap`/`locationIdByName`), `AnchorsScreen` (`blockMap`/`tierMap`), `GroupsScreen` (`tierMap`) and `buildAttendance.js` (`tierIdByNameKey`), all via the same `mapWithCollisions`. The three screens use the per-row `warning` string the preview already renders — a warned row already skips at commit, so setting a warning IS the refusal, and an ambiguous location specifically must not fall through to the create branch or it would mint a THIRD same-named place. `buildAttendance` gains a third `ambiguous` state beside `unmatched` (a name that resolves to NOTHING is a spreadsheet typo; one that resolves to TWO is a division to rename — different remedies, different screens) and falls back to the never-unplaced rule R1 rather than to an arbitrary tier. **Slice C** closes the remaining two with the OTHER of T255's two answers — a deterministic lowest-id tie-break, via the new `src/lib/nameIdTiebreak.js` (`lowestIdOf`), for resolution with no human in the loop: create-time locations dedupe (`src/lib/locationDedup.js`, whose copy in `SpecialEventsScreen` is folded away) picked whatever came first in React-state array order, and the locations migration-review gate re-derived an id from a name that `listMigrationReviews` already SELECTs. The same slice makes `listAliasMap` deterministic (it had no `ORDER BY`, so which of two colliding aliases won was up to SQLite) and stops it merging two Programs' alias namespaces when no Program is active. The import tier dropdown is spun out to **T257**: it is a capability gap rather than a wrong bind — the commit side is already deterministic, but a director cannot reach the second same-named division, and carrying an id there is blocked by proposed tiers having no id and by an id leaking into reconciliation copy. The Locations export sheet's missing `shoresh_id` was **deferred by owner decision** to T256: the gap predates v73, fixing it is a coordinated both-sides change plus a shape change to workbooks directors already hold, and `locations` already carries a duplicate marker and merge verb so the case is surfaced today. docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:211:_Prior: 2026-09-18 (**The participant data substrate lands — schema v66, seven new synced entities, and the first records this app has ever held about identifiable children (T194).** `campers`, `elective_assignment_runs`, `elective_occurrences`, `elective_choices`, `elective_choice_offerings`, `elective_preferences` and `elective_assignments` are registered end to end: `schema.sql`, the v66 migration and `rollback/v66_down.js`, `PROJECTIONS`, `DIRECT_CAMP_ENTITIES`/`PARENT_SCOPED_ENTITIES`/`DOMAIN_SNAPSHOT_ORDER`, the Automerge document's `MODELED_ENTITIES` and a regenerated genesis, `RESTORE_DECISIONS`, `UNDO_REFERENCE_CHECKS` and the mock write allowlist. **No rendered surface ships in this slice** — the solver (T196), the preference importer (T195) and the export (T197) are separate tickets. **The one invariant that matters is ADR D4: an assignment's id is DERIVED from `(run_id, camper_id, occurrence_id)`** via `electron/ops/electiveDerivedIds.js`, because `UNIQUE_FIELD_ENTITIES` expresses only single-column uniqueness and a composite cannot be registered. Without it, two devices generating the same draft run mint two rows with different ids — two independent Automerge map keys, so the merge records no conflict, both survive, and a camper is silently assigned twice. The derivation is length-prefixed concatenation, not a hash: provably injective, debuggable in a SQLite shell, and dependency-free so the migration can load it. It lives under `electron/` because electron-builder does not package `src/`. Proved by a real two-device merge (`test/integration/scenarios/31-derived-id-convergence.automerge.js`) rather than by a unit test — the duplicate it forbids is a MERGE phenomenon — and verified non-vacuous by swapping the derivation for `randomUUID`, which turns it red with "got 2". **Capacity becomes a two-part value** (`elective_set_activities.capacity_mode` + `capacity_limit`, ADR D3) so "uncapped" and "closed" are no longer the same number; `camper_headcount` is retained in the table but retired from the write path. The CHECKs are deliberately PER-COLUMN and **not** the obvious cross-column pairing: the projection writes one field per op, and both orderings traverse a state a pairing CHECK forbids, which would fail on the RECEIVING device during sync replay. A named test pins that. **The whole domain is admin-only** (ADR D9): all seven are deliberately kept OUT of `permissions.js` `ENTITIES`, which derives staff read AND write with no per-entity opt-in, and `participantEntitiesAdminOnly.test.js` asserts the NEGATIVE, since the existing parity test guards omission and cannot catch an over-grant. History and Trash on them turned out to be admin-only **already**, by two independent existing mechanisms rather than a new one — `getEntityHistoryHandler` authorizes the per-entity `<entity>.read`, and `listDeleted` filters to `RESTORABLE_ENTITIES`, which none of the seven is. Those `RESTORE_DECISIONS` refusals are therefore a **security boundary**, not just a product decision. `recordAuditEvent` now REFUSES free text in metadata for these entities (`audit_events` is append-only and survives every purge, T202's included). Deleting a schedule week with runs attached is **blocked** with a message naming them (owner ruling), rather than orphaning the run or silently destroying a finalized roster. **Genesis was regenerated, invalidating every existing `.automerge` file and requiring every paired device to re-pair** — free only because the owner confirmed on 2026-09-17 that the project is pre-production and no real camp document exists. **A document row that cannot be projected is now contained per ROW and recorded, not merely logged.** `electron/automerge/projector.js`'s `upsertRow` wraps one record's whole field loop in a SAVEPOINT, so a row whose earlier fields apply but whose later field violates a CHECK is rolled all the way back rather than left half-written — the partial-row state the earlier per-FIELD containment allowed. The drop writes one `projection_failures` row, so a missing camper assignment is repairable rather than visible only in a console nobody watches; recording is itself contained, and the contained rows are reported to the app through `onProjectionError` after the transaction so a director can still learn of them. **That recording no longer fabricates anything.** `projection_failures.op_id` carried a foreign key to `operations(id)`, which a document-native failure cannot honestly satisfy — nothing was written, so there is no event to point at — and the first implementation minted a synthetic `operations` row to satisfy it. A third `store` value, `'document-replay'`, now identifies this failure kind, and its `op_id` is derived deterministically from `entity`/`entity_id`/`field`: it names the fact of the failure rather than an invented write, and repeated failures collapse onto one row through the `ON CONFLICT` idiom the other two writers already use. Nothing is inserted into `operations` or `devices`. **v66 REBUILDS `projection_failures`** (copy/drop/rename, preserving every row, the fresh-database column order and `idx_projection_failures_unresolved`) because the table dates from v55 and `schema.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` cannot drop a constraint on a database that already has it — editing the declaration alone left the old foreign key in place on every existing database, where the new id violated it and containment made that failure silent. `repairProjectionForEntity` refuses an entity holding an unresolved `document-replay` row rather than inferring document-ownership from the op log's shape; its remedy is re-projecting from the document, never op-log replay. See the 2026-09-17 addendum to `docs/adr/2026-09-04-projection-failure-detection-and-recovery.md`. Because a document-native entity's field writes never reach the `operations` table, `repairProjectionForEntity` now REFUSES an entity whose op set holds no real field op instead of taking its clean-rebuild branch and marking the failure resolved — that path would have reported HEALTHY on a device silently missing the row. The resolution that is correct lives in the projector: a row that successfully projects clears its own outstanding failure. `getEntityHistory` now filters to real columns plus the fields it already special-cases, so no non-column op row reaches the director-facing history panel. **The soft-reference discipline covers the seven NEW participant tables only** — `anchor_activities.schedule_week_id` and `elective_sets.schedule_week_id` keep the real `REFERENCES schedule_weeks(id)` they shipped with. **T195 adds the preference import service on top of the T194 substrate — bounded CSV/XLSX parse, a closed column-mapping enum, pure identity/choice resolution, read-only preview, and an op-log commit.** `src/ingest/preferenceImport/{parseSheet,mapping,resolve}.js` are pure (no db, no I/O); `electron/ops/{preferenceImportPreview,commitPreferenceImport}.js` are the only db-touching halves. Identity/activity matching is EXACT STRING EQUALITY throughout — no casing/whitespace/fuzzy auto-merge — and every ambiguous row BLOCKS with a reason from a closed enum rather than guessing; `AMBIGUOUS_SAME_GROUP` carries existing-camper candidates so a director resolves it in `src/screens/PreferenceImportScreen.jsx` (admin-only, fixture-data-only pending the owner's dated acceptance of real camper data) without a database edit. Preview is asserted read-only by two independent mechanisms (a `db.prepare` spy and a live row-count snapshot). Commit re-runs the resolver against the CURRENT snapshot inside its own `runAtomic` before writing anything — a preview whose facts drifted aborts the whole batch as `PREVIEW_STALE` with zero rows written — and every field write is idempotent per `(client_write_id, entity, entity_id, field)`, so a retried commit re-applies cleanly rather than duplicating. **No schema change beyond v66** was needed. T195 writes `elective_occurrences` for the run (an owner ruling: the occurrence fingerprint is what makes `STALE_OUTER_SCHEDULE` detectable) but never `elective_assignments` — that is T196 entirely. A `scripts/preferenceImportCli.js` exposes preview only; commit is not CLI-reachable (T198's scope). Prior header note follows.)_

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:222:_Prior: 2026-09-17 (**A headless caller can no longer be told a schedule is clean when it is not, and over-capacity between two AUTHORED overlays is now detected at all — T193, no schema change.** Two independent defects, both pre-existing on shipped surfaces. docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:224:_Prior: 2026-09-16 (**A schema index now survives the table rebuild that used to drop it — T189, no schema version change.** `schema.sql` is exec'd at the START of every `openLocalDb()`, before the migrations, so its `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` statements had already run by the time migrations v53 and v59 rebuilt `schedule_snapshots` with `DROP TABLE` + `RENAME` — which took `idx_schedule_snapshots_template_id` with it. Every fresh database therefore ran its first session without that index (25 declared indexes on open #1, 26 from open #2 onward), as did every database upgrading across v53 or v59. Impact was performance only — a table scan on `schedule_snapshots` for one launch — and no stored data was wrong, which is why no schema version was bumped: the defect lived inside a single open and `schema.sql` restored the index on the next one. Both blocks now re-create the index immediately after their `RENAME` from a shared `SCHEDULE_SNAPSHOTS_TEMPLATE_ID_INDEX_DDL` constant, byte-identical to the `schema.sql` line. The more durable half is the guard: 33 `electron/db/*.migration.test.js` files assert fresh-vs-migrated equivalence and none caught this, because the twelve that check indexes do so per-table and by hand enrolment, and nobody enrolled `schedule_snapshots`. `schemaIndexParity.migration.test.js` replaces that with whole-database parity of every declared non-table object between a fresh file's first open and its second, so no future table needs remembering. PR-2 closes it with the owner's chosen preserve-and-report: BEFORE the teardown, each division-scoped anchor's `unit_ids` is snapshotted as division NAMES keyed by a stable label/name key (`divisionPreserveKey` — day label + block name + event name + cohort, because `anchorSlotKey`'s day_id/time_block_id are themselves recreated with new ids and cannot survive the teardown); on recreate, those names re-resolve to the NEW tier ids through the importer's existing `tierIdByName` map, and a fully-resolved event is rewritten as `unit_ids` + `is_all_groups=0` + empty `group_ids` (the AnchorsScreen shape, so the two scope columns never disagree). A division the new file no longer contains — or an event that moved off its slot — cannot be re-resolved and is surfaced on `outcome.fixedEvents.scopeFlattened` rather than lost silently; preserved ones report on `scopePreserved`. Add-mode was never affected (no teardown). This completes T183; anchor scope is now consistent on BOTH the read and write sides. Prior header note follows.)_

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:228:_Prior: 2026-09-16 (**An activity pinned for SOME groups on SOME days stays schedulable for the others — no schema change (ADR §10 Q5).** The engine's anchored-activity exclusion is now keyed by GROUP × DAY rather than by group. docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:230:_Prior: 2026-09-16 (**A dismissed schedule finding is now content-addressed by its material payload, so a genuinely worse finding at the same coordinates is no longer masked (T185, no schema change).** A finding dismissal on `ScheduleScreen` lives in ephemeral per-route state (`dismissedByRoute`, never persisted) and was keyed by `groupId|activityId|kind` only. A dismissal thus covers exactly the finding dismissed: it survives incidental edits that leave it identical, and a materially-changed or genuinely-new finding at the same coordinates resurfaces. docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:236:_Prior: 2026-09-16 (**Fixed Events are matched to their activity BY NAME, so an already-pinned activity is no longer scheduled a second time — no schema change (T62, reopened).** An anchor row (`anchor_activities`) has never had an `activity_id` column: the Anchors screen has asked the director to TYPE the event since the first commit, and `electron/ops/ingest.js` writes a name and no activity link. The unit test shipped with that fix hand-built an anchor carrying `activity_id`, so it asserted the fix's own premise and stayed green for a month. New `src/engine/anchorActivityLink.js` is now the single place the link is resolved, keyed on the repo's existing recognition key (`whitespaceInsensitiveName` — lowercase, whitespace-stripped), re-spelled locally so the engine keeps its no-dependency purity and guarded by `anchorActivityLink.keyParity.test.js`, which fails if the two spellings ever diverge. `buildSchedule.js` scopes the exclusion PER GROUP (a group-scoped Recurring event must not remove its activity from every other group's catalogue) and `weekCatalog.js` uses the same resolver, fixing a second, unreported instance: its `activity_id ?? unit_id` read compared a TIER id against ACTIVITY ids, so closing an activity or a location for a week never suppressed that activity's anchor. **A consequence worth knowing before reading a generated schedule:** the corrected exclusion removes placements that were silently there, so unfillable blocks rose 135 -> 195 on the owner's camp — a real catalogue shortfall that the phantom placements had been masking, now visible instead of hidden. The anchor's NAME is therefore load-bearing for SCHEDULING and not merely for display: anything that re-keys it (a name-variant merge, a compound-cell resolution "Lunch + Leave" -> "Lunch") changes scheduling eligibility. `docs/adr/2026-09-12-activities-as-one-entity-with-placement.md` remains PROPOSED and unapproved; it had already recorded this defect at its §3.2 and proposes a real `activity_id` FK as the eventual model — this fix does not foreclose that, and the resolver prefers an explicit `activity_id` the moment one exists. Its §10 Q5 (may an activity pinned for some groups still rotate for the others?) is answered YES and is being implemented separately as group x day keying. Prior header note follows.)_
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:277:`src/ingest/coScheduleRules.js` gives every activity its own co-scheduling rule from observed co-occurrence — `can_co_schedule`, `max_groups_per_slot`, and WHICH groups: Lunch 1 seen with Tzofim 1/2/3 every day is co-schedulable for those three and no one else, while a Sports never seen with two groups at once cannot share. The rule belongs to the ACTIVITY, so an all-camp anchor gets its own (large) rule too; anchors are excluded only from the DIVISION inference, where their co-occurrence would wrongly imply shared membership. `same_tier_only` follows once membership is known.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:281:**Co-schedule now reaches the database and records why** (same day, follow-up commit). `inferCoScheduleRules` had been running at parse time with its output assigned to a ref nothing read — every activity's observed capacity was computed and discarded. It now travels as `rule.co_schedule` onto `activities.max_groups_per_slot` / `same_tier_only`, along TWO paths that are both required: a CREATE item carries `fields: {}` (buildPlan builds creates from the `_rule` side-channel, handled in `commitCreate`) while an UPDATE carries real `fields` (handled in `foldApprovedToRecords`, `src/ingest/fieldUpdate.js`) — wiring either alone writes nothing on first import or never refreshes on re-import. `import_evidence` rows back both fields: `max_groups_per_slot` is `observed`/`high` (a count of groups in one slot is seen, not deduced), `same_tier_only` is `inferred`/`low` because it rests on a group->division map itself inferred from group names. `co_schedule_groups` lives in the evidence `support` rather than a column — it is the observation behind the constraint, not a constraint the engine reads — so no schema change was needed. `RULE_FIELDS` (`src/utils/ruleProvenance.js`) gains a fourth row, giving the Activities screen's Co-schedule column the same provenance dot and confirm gesture as the other three. **The schedule importer now declines a workbook that is not a schedule** (T146). Picking the wrong file out of a folder used to produce a reconciliation screen full of one-character "activities" (`L`, `M`, `S`, `3`, `7`) from a campus-map template's grid coordinates, presented with the same confidence as real extractions. `src/ingest/scheduleShape.js`'s `isScheduleShaped(pages)` requires POSITIVE evidence of a schedule — a day axis (most columns are day names, or the page title names a day) or a time axis (most row labels look like a clock time) — rather than a blocklist of sheet names, which renaming the file would evade. One qualifying page anywhere accepts the whole set, biased toward acceptance because a false reject blocks a real camp's real file. It lives in its own module imported only by `ImportScreen.jsx` and runs in `readFiles` BEFORE `extractEntities`, so no partial extraction is possible — and so the per-entity template importers (Locations, Electives, Special Events, germination), which take non-schedule workbooks by design, never reach it. **Divisions now carry evidence too**, closing that asymmetry: `divisionSupportByGroup` records per group the basis (`name_stem` / `solo` / `split_by_co_occurrence`), the stem that clustered it, its siblings, whether a bracketed qualifier was stripped, and which anchors were excluded — the last two load-bearing, since `Tzofim 1 (girls)` clusters only because the bracket came off and an all-camp lunch left in would stop anything ever splitting. Stored as `import_evidence` on **groups/`tier_id`**, always `inferred`, `high` confidence for a split (the grid corroborates it) and `low` for a bare name cluster. A division the file STATED is excluded — a stated fact is not an inference. The **Groups** screen renders a quiet provenance dot (mirroring LocationsScreen's capacity dot) whose sentence comes from `describeDivisionEvidence` (`src/utils/divisionProvenance.js`); it disappears once a director re-assigns the division by hand. New IPC `listDivisionEvidence` (`electron/main.js` + preload + `localClient`), with the browser-dev mock degrading to no evidence. The quiet dot-and-popover chrome shared by Groups and Locations now lives in one place, `src/components/setup/ProvenanceDot.jsx` (plus `provenanceDotStyles.js`), after a third near-copy appeared — it is the SINGLE-field shape and deliberately not a replacement for ActivitiesScreen's N-row `RuleProvenanceDot`. `weather_alternative_id` remains uninferred and is now formally downscoped — "what to run when it rains" is a plan the director holds, and a schedule records no weather, so any correlation drawn from the grid would be manufactured. `is_outdoor` was dropped from scope entirely: it is a property of the PLACE, not the placement, and no arrangement of schedule cells can establish it (spun off as T147). Prior header note follows.)_

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:307:_Prior: 2026-09-03 (**Ingest asks the director to interpret compound schedule cells (T118) — schema v54.** A new pure classifier (`src/ingest/compoundCellPatterns.js`) detects cells like "Lunch + Leave" or "Change/Snack" that a naive importer previously read as their own standalone activity — silently fabricating a scheduling rule for something that isn't real (verified end-to-end: one real camp file produced 2,233 spurious UNDERSERVED findings this way). Numbered past a RESERVED v67 (an unmerged worktree elsewhere already committed a `v67_down.js` for a different change) — the banded guard is `>= 66 && < 68`, the true predecessor version in this tree, not `>= 67`. **v67 added `device_identity_key`** (T162) — a per-device persistent Ed25519 libp2p transport identity, host-local and never replicated, which makes a device's PeerId stable across restarts and is the precondition for binding a session token to the peer presenting it (`peer_identity_mismatch`, close code 4405). The migration also NULLs every `devices.libp2p_peer_id` so each device re-binds by trust-on-first-use. `rollback/v67_down.js` drops the table and states plainly that the nulled values are not restorable. **v66 added the participant data substrate** (T194): the seven tables `campers`/`elective_assignment_runs`/`elective_occurrences`/`elective_choices`/`elective_choice_offerings`/`elective_preferences`/`elective_assignments`, plus `elective_set_activities.capacity_mode` and `.capacity_limit` (ALTER-appended LAST, the column-order trap, with a backfill from `camper_headcount` that is provably a no-op on every database surveyed). All seven are NEW tables created by identical `CREATE TABLE` text on both the fresh and migrated paths, so column order is equal by construction rather than by care. No table rebuild anywhere, so `elective_set_activities`' UNIQUE index is never dropped (the T189 failure class, avoided by not entering it). `rollback/v66_down.js` exists and states plainly that it is **not a purge**: it drops the projected rows but leaves the op-log and the Automerge document untouched. The same change regenerated the Automerge `GENESIS_B64`, invalidating every existing `.automerge` file. **Correction to a prior note:** this paragraph previously attributed v66 to a `UNIQUE(camp_id, day_of_week)` index on `days_of_operation` (T203). That migration was withdrawn before merge — the T203 header note above says so, and `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stayed at 65 — so v66 was never taken and is T194's. The `days_of_operation` uniqueness work is re-ticketed as T205 and will take a later version. **v59 DROPPED the `day_overrides` table and the `schedule_snapshots.day_overrides_json` column** (T145) — a hard cutover discarding the data, following the v53/overlay-retirement precedent; `rollback/v59_down.js` restores the shape and says plainly it cannot restore the data. **v58 added `projection_failures.store`** — distinguishing an op that failed to reach SQLite (replayable from the op-log) from one that reached SQLite but not the Automerge document, where that replay would be wrong because SQLite is already correct and the document is the one behind (see `docs/current/WHERE_DATA_LIVES.md`). **v57 added `devices.libp2p_peer_id`** (Stage 5d-2a) — no longer a dormant column; the Automerge/libp2p sync engine it belongs to is now the live default (see header note and Stack). **v54 added `compound_cell_decisions`** (T118, see above); **v55 added `projection_failures`** (see above); **v56 added `location_word_decisions`** (see above). **v48–v53 (lifecycle-IA / Roots-home / Special-Events / WS5 program, landed 2026-08-27→2026-08-30):** v48 added tile-world placement columns on `locations` (dormant spatial-layer seam, unrelated UI already removed at this point); v49 renamed `locations.tile_type` → `kind` and expanded its allowed-value set (same dormant seam); v50 introduced an indoor/outdoor `camp_maps` pair per camp plus a per-location map reference (also dormant — see the header note and Removed/Replaced); v51 added **`anchor_activities.kind`** (Fixed vs Recurring, table-recreate migration — see above); v52 added the **`open_reconciliation_decisions`** table (see above); v53 **dropped `template_overlays`** and `schedule_snapshots.overlays` (see above). Each v48–v51 table-recreate migration follows the same guarded, FK-pragma-restored, exception-safe (try/finally) shape; a rollback (`v4N_down.js`/`v5N_down.js`) exists through v53. **v39–v47 (unified schedule-overlay program):** v39 added `elective_set_activities.camper_headcount` (per-offering elective capacity); v40 added the **`events`** table + `template_slots.event_id`; v41 added the event internal-sub-schedule tables `event_time_blocks`/`event_groups`/`event_slots`; v42 added `anchor_activities.schedule_week_id` + `recurrence_level` (recurrence axis); v43 added six binding columns on `elective_sets` (`day_id`/`time_block_id`/`is_all_groups`/`group_ids`/`schedule_week_id`/`recurrence_level`); v44 added `activities.recurrence_truth_status`; v45 added `anchor_activities.location_id` + `events.location_id` (engine location-contention); v46 **DROPPED** the dead `day_override_templates`/`day_override_template_slots` pair (writer-less since T108); v47 added the host-local **`declined_two_row_splits`** table. Each v39–v45 ALTER appends its column LAST (column-order trap). Rollbacks `v3N_down.js`/`v4N_down.js` exist through v47 (`v46_down.js` deletes `schema_migrations WHERE version >= 46`, not `= 46`, so a stacked-migration rollback can't strand a higher version). v32 (M5) added `week_location_exclusions`; v33 (M6) added `camp_maps`; v34 (T40) added the `special_days`/`special_day_time_blocks`/`special_day_slots` family; v35 (T41) added `elective_sets`/`elective_set_activities` and the `template_slots.elective_set_id` column; v36 (T110) added `elective_sets.is_reusable`; v37 (T106) added `special_days.notes`; v38 (T108) added the `day_overrides` table + `schedule_snapshots.day_overrides_json`. Each of v36–v38 ALTER-adds its column LAST on a migrated db (the column-order trap noted in `schema.sql`). Rollbacks `v3N_down.js` exist for the recent ones. v19 added `audit_events`; v20 added device trust columns (`authorized_at`, `authorized_by_user_id`, `revoked_at`, `revoked_by_user_id`, `revocation_reason`, `device_secret_identifier`, `pairing_status`) to `devices`, the `host_signing_key` singleton table, and `camps.signing_public_key`. v26–v29 (reconciliation/onboarding program): v27 added **`schedule_weeks`** (`id, camp_id, name, sort_order, is_archived`) and `schedule_templates.week_id` (a template now belongs to a week, `UNIQUE(week_id, kind)` replacing the old per-camp uniqueness); v28 added **`week_activity_exclusions`** (`id, week_id, activity_id`, unique per week/activity) and **`week_group_exclusions`** (`id, week_id, group_id`, unique per week/group); v29 added **`operations.source`** (nullable `TEXT`) — per-field provenance (import vs. hand-edit) backing the S2a/S2b field-update-and-hand-edit-protection design. v30 (S1b) added the host-local **`source_aliases`** table (see above) + rollback `electron/db/rollback/v30_down.js`; v31 (B4) added the host-local **`import_evidence`** table (see above), also absent from every sync registry.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:764:- **elective_sets** / **elective_set_activities** + **template_slots.elective_set_id** — schema **v35** (T41 slice 1, design `docs/work/specs/2026-08-20-group-electives-design.md`). **Group-level elective scheduling** (no campers, no solver — human decides, app holds it). `elective_sets` (`id, camp_id, name, sort_order, is_reusable`, `UNIQUE(camp_id, name)`) is a named set of activity options ("Afternoon Chugim"); the **`is_reusable`** column (schema **v36**, T110) is the durability marker distinguishing a reusable set from a one-off (pre-v36 rows default reusable). `elective_set_activities` (`id, elective_set_id, activity_id`, parent-scoped join) its members. An elective is a **kind of cell content** — a nullable `elective_set_id` on the existing `template_slots` (mutually exclusive with `activity_id`) — inside the normal slot/route/sync machinery, NOT a parallel grid. The **engine SKIPS elective cells** (excluded from both head-selection and the span-tail collision check, mirroring anchors — `src/engine/buildSchedule.js` `electiveLookup`, threaded via `useGeneration.js`). `deleteElectiveSet.js` cascades. **Inline authoring now shipped** (T110/PR #132): a director creates/places an elective set directly from an empty schedule cell (point-of-intent), including the cross-device atomic-create invariant. **Ingest wiring shipped** (T195, schema **v68**): `elective_set_activities.status` (`'potential'|'confirmed'`, default `'confirmed'`) distinguishes an imported-but-unconfirmed offering from everything else. `src/ingest/parseGridSchedule.js`'s `parseGridScheduleMenu` + `src/ingest/electiveSetPopulate.js`'s `populateElectiveGrid`/`populateElectiveSet` import a day×period grid of offering MENU cells, minting/reusing one `elective_sets` row per `(day_id, time_block_id, schedule_week_id)` and never overwriting a `'confirmed'` row. A `'potential'` row is filtered at the schedule engine, conflicts, and export boundaries but stays visible on both authoring screens (`ElectiveSetDetail.jsx`, `ScheduleElectivesScreen.jsx`) for a director to confirm.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:770:- **compound_cell_decisions** — schema **v54** (T118, ADR `docs/adr/2026-09-03-compound-cell-interpretation.md`). `id, camp_id, pattern, interpretation ('as_written'|'wrapper'|'alternatives'), anchor_name?, wrapper_name?, confirmed_by?, confirmed_at`, `UNIQUE(camp_id, pattern)`. **Host-local, never synced** — same trust model as `source_aliases`/`import_evidence`/`declined_two_row_splits`. Durable memory of a director's confirmed interpretation of a compound schedule cell (e.g. "Lunch + Leave" = a wrapper around "Lunch"), so a later import at the same camp resolves the pattern silently instead of asking again. Single writer `electron/ops/confirmCompoundCellPattern.js` (host-only, admin-gated at the IPC boundary). Read at import time via `localClient` and threaded into the pure `extractEntities`/`fixedEvents`/`multiBlockCandidates` extraction functions as a plain lookup argument (no db access added to those functions themselves).

docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:774:- **`fixed_events.unit_ids`** (the table was `anchor_activities` until T267/v77) — schema **v65** (T180, division scope stored rather than snapshotted). `TEXT`, a JSON array of tier ids: the age DIVISIONS a Recurring Event is scoped to. Resolved LIVE at build time by `resolveAnchorGroupIds` (`src/engine/anchorScope.js`), so a group added to one of those divisions later is covered without the event being re-saved — which is the whole point. Table-recreate migration, because v51's cross-column CHECK grew a fourth clause (a `kind='fixed'` row must also have `unit_ids` NULL or `'[]'`). Backfill converts a legacy singular `unit_id` to a one-element list; a `group_ids` snapshot row is deliberately NOT converted. `unit_id` remains as a read-only pre-v65 fallback — the engine still resolves it, nothing writes it. **`group_ids` is therefore no longer a complete answer to "which groups does this anchor cover"**: for a division-scoped event it is empty and correct, and every reader must go through the resolver. **T183 finished that consolidation on both sides.** `src/engine/anchorScope.js` now holds the whole family sharing one precedence: `resolveAnchorGroupIds` (group-id projection, engine + `weekCatalog` + T182's finding seam), `resolveAnchorUnitIds` (the DIVISION projection for the Anchors label, flagging a lossy legacy `group_ids`-derived answer `inferred` rather than presenting it as stored scope), and `resolveAnchorDayIds` (day coverage, Q5). The two former column-readers now route through it: `AnchorsScreen.anchorTierLabel` (was inline) and `electron/ops/ingest.js`'s `liveAnchorScope` drift check (which had reported a spurious "scope changed" for division-scoped rows). And the WRITE side is closed: a **Replace re-import now PRESERVES a director's `unit_ids`** instead of flattening it to a group snapshot — `ingest.js` snapshots the division names before `replaceScope`'s teardown (keyed by day-label/block-name/event-name/cohort, since the id-based slot key can't survive the tier/day/block recreation), re-resolves them to the recreated tier ids, and reports on `outcome.fixedEvents.scopeFlattened` any division it genuinely cannot carry across (renamed/removed, or a partly-rematched multi-day event) rather than losing it silently.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:775:- **`fixed_events.kind`** (the table was `anchor_activities` until T267/v77) — schema **v51** (Fixed vs Recurring un-conflation, WS2, ADR `docs/adr/2026-08-28-fixed-vs-recurring-events.md`). `TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'fixed' CHECK (kind IN ('fixed','recurring'))`, with a cross-column CHECK (`kind='fixed'` requires `is_all_groups=1 AND unit_id IS NULL AND group_ids` empty). SQLite can't attach a cross-column CHECK via `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, so the migration recreates the table (same shape as the v48–v50 `locations`/`camp_maps` rewrites). Backfill is classification-only, not a free default: an existing all-camp row backfills `'fixed'`, every group/division-scoped row backfills `'recurring'` — zero engine change (ADR §4). Powers the sidebar's two `AnchorsScreen` rows (`fixedevents`/`anchors`, see Screens).
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:790:- **Per-week availability (M5)**: `week_location_exclusions` (`week_id, location_id`) marks a location closed for a specific week — "the lake is closed weeks 1 and 2" — mirroring the existing `week_activity_exclusions`/`week_group_exclusions` pattern. Honored on both schedule-building routes (manual and generated) plus at anchor placement, matching how activity/group week exclusions are already honored (see `docs/adr/2026-08-17-...` history and the manual-route week-exclusions work).
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:796:- **Referrer completeness (2026-09-05)**: merging or deleting a location now re-points (merge) or nulls (delete) all six referrer kinds — `activities.location_id`, `week_location_exclusions.location_id`, `anchor_activities.location_id`, `events.location_id`, `special_day_slots.location_id`, `event_slots.location_id` — in the same transaction; previously only the first two were handled and the other four were silently orphaned. `DeleteRecordDialog` now shows a breakdown by kind instead of a bare count.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:834:**Machine-facing surface (not an HTTP API).** A headless **MCP server** (`scripts/mcp/server.js` + `tools.js`, launch `npm run mcp -- --db <path>`, stdio, read-only by default with an `--allow-write` gate; ADR `docs/adr/2026-08-21-mcp-ingestion-server.md`, plan `docs/work/plans/2026-09-01-machine-access.md`) exposes domain verbs over a camp's SQLite file with no Electron/IPC/auth: `ingest_preview`, `ingest_commit`, `list_entities`, `setup_summary`, `schedule_state` (**the validate verb** — re-runs the pure engine over stored placement, moving nothing. `findings` are what the app's Schedule screen shows for that same placement; `conflicts` are **not** — no screen renders them today, so this tool is presently the only place a caller sees them. As of T193 the `conflicts` array is genuinely computed (`src/engine/routeConflicts.js`, called from `src/engine/buildSchedule.js:879`): it re-derives combined location occupancy across regular activities, anchors, events and elective offerings, and reports `OUTER_RESOURCE_CONFLICT` wherever occupancy at one location/day/block exceeds that location's capacity — both across cohorts and within a single one. It is reporting-only and never moves a group or removes an offering. So an empty `conflicts` result now means "checked and clear" **for location-capacity contention, which is the only kind it evaluates** — it is not a general all-clear: a slot whose content carries no `location_id`, and a `location_id` that resolves to nothing in `locations`, are both treated as unconstrained and are not checked, deliberately matching `placeBlocked` in `buildSchedule.js`), `export_schedule` (the stable **`format_version: 1`** JSON schedule export), `get_elective_assignment_run` and `export_elective_assignments` (T198 — read-only, camp-scoped reads of one elective assignment run and its versioned export document, built from the same shared assembly the app's own export uses; there is deliberately no `generate` verb), and two support/debugging tools added 2026-09-04 for the projection-failures ledger (see Database Tables): `check_projection_health` (read-only, always available — lists this device's unresolved projection failures) and `repair_projection_entity` (`--allow-write`-gated — re-derives one entity's row from its full op-log history). Neither of the latter two is director-facing. The same portable JSON export is available in the GUI (`src/utils/exportScheduleJson.js`'s `buildScheduleExport`, shared with the Excel export via `src/utils/scheduleCells.js` so the two can't drift; "Export data (JSON)" on the Schedule toolbar). The libp2p sync protocol is device-to-device plumbing, deliberately **not** exposed as a public API.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:854:- **Fixed events route to the schedule, not the activity catalog** (2026-08-09, ADR `docs/adr/2026-08-09-ingest-fixed-event-routing-and-reviewable-units.md`): a name pinned across the grid (Lunch, Mifkad, Sign-In) was being written BOTH as a catalog `activities` row and as `anchor_activities`. It now defaults OUT of the catalog. Critically, this is a **visible, reversible review decision**, not a silent engine drop — `inferFixedEvents` returns `dualUseNames` only as a SEED for the import review's tick-defaults (pin-only rows default unticked with a note + one-click override; genuinely dual-use names default kept, flagged). `buildPlan`/`commitPlan` are unchanged and golden-ops stay byte-identical. This deliberately avoids letting the fixed-event detector's intentional over-inclusion silently delete a legitimate catalog activity.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:933:**Ingest asks the director to interpret compound schedule cells (T118, schema v54, 2026-09-03).** ADR `docs/adr/2026-09-03-compound-cell-interpretation.md`. Pressure-testing ingestion against real camp files surfaced a class of cell — `Lunch + Leave`, `Change/Snack`, `Climbing/Sports` — that the importer previously read wrong with HIGH (silent) confidence, since the confidence model conflated "seen reliably" with "understood correctly": one real file produced a fabricated "min 6 / max 8 times per week" rule for a cell that was never a real activity, cascading into 2,233 spurious UNDERSERVED findings. Four slices shipped: (1) a new pure classifier (`src/ingest/compoundCellPatterns.js`) flags a candidate compound pattern using a partner-diversity test (a word pairing with ≥2 distinct partners is a wrapper/rotation candidate; a word that only ever pairs with the same partner, e.g. "Arts & Crafts", is one fixed name — no card generated) plus a standalone-anchor test to guess which side is the real activity; (2) a new host-local, never-synced `compound_cell_decisions` table (schema v54) persists a confirmed interpretation per camp, sibling to `source_aliases`/`declined_two_row_splits`; (3) the camp's confirmed decisions are read at parse time and threaded into the pure `extractEntities`/`fixedEvents`/`multiBlockCandidates` functions as a plain lookup, exactly parallel to the existing `canonicalMap` pattern — no db access added to those functions; (4) a new "Cells We Weren't Sure About" section on `ImportScreen.jsx` (same visual family as "Longer Blocks", nothing pre-selected) asks the director once per distinct pattern with four choices — one thing as written / a wrapper around [anchor] / co-equal alternatives / not sure yet — and a resolved "wrapper" verdict keeps the wrapper string out of the activity catalog and fabricates no rule for it. Never auto-splits or auto-merges a cell; a genuinely ambiguous wrapper-direction guess is withheld rather than guessed (follow-up fix after the initial slice). Out of scope for v1: the separate PDF-multi-line-merge parsing gap, and PDF import itself (not supported today).
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:936:- **Import materializes a saved version** (ADR `docs/adr/2026-09-02-imported-schedule-materializes-as-a-version.md`, T117 slices 1–2). `src/ingest/capturePlacements.js` (slice 1) walks the parsed import grid and returns the actual (group, day, block) → activity/anchor cells it sees; `electron/ops/resolveImportedPlacements.js` + `electron/ops/materializeImportedVersion.js` (slice 2) turn those into ONE `schedule_snapshots` row — named "Imported schedule", on the manual route's `schedule_templates` — written host-side immediately after `commitIngest` returns (not inside its transaction, so `commitIngest`'s six-entity create whitelist stays untouched). The imported version never auto-fills the working grid; a director pulls it up from the Schedule screen's version history like any hand-saved snapshot ("grid = workbench, versions = woodpile"). A placement whose name can't be resolved against the just-committed catalog is counted and reported on `ImportScreen.jsx`'s post-commit banner, not silently dropped. Re-importing (Add or Replace) always adds another version rather than replacing the last one. Three v1 limitations are recorded in the ADR as accepted, not fixed: anchor-first name resolution on a dual-use name, an anchor's day/group scope isn't verified against the cell it lands in, and a narrow first-import concurrency window when the manual template row must be minted.
docs/current/PLATFORM_STATE.md:937:- **Restore-time reference guard** (same window, Red Hat-flagged HIGH). `useSnapshots.js`'s `restoreSnapshot` now filters out any saved slot whose group/day/time-block/activity/anchor id no longer exists in the live catalog before writing it back to the grid, reporting a skipped-cell count ("N cell(s)...were skipped") to the director — closing a gap where a Replace re-import (which mints new catalog ids) could make restoring an older version silently write a broken/blank grid. Applies to every saved version, imported or hand-made.

docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:480:Anchoring to `gridContent` rather than to the outer screen is deliberate: §5b says *"pinned to the top of the affected panel."* The grid is the panel being regenerated; the controls bar above it stays interactive and must not appear to be under progress.
docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:556:### File and anchor
docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:594:| Type | **Pop in** — scale + fade, anchored |
docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:602:**Why 180ms and not a token.** The rail sits between the token tiers: `--motion-fast` (140ms) is the micro-state tier and undersells a whole panel arriving; `--motion-base` (220ms) is the panel tier but the rail is trigger-anchored and retriggerable, where 220ms starts to feel like waiting. 180ms is the audit's specified value and is retained verbatim. **Maker: do not substitute a token here.** If Governor prefers strict token discipline over the audit's value, that is a standard-amendment question (a fourth duration token), not a Maker choice — flag it, ship 180ms.
docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:604:**Why `transform-origin: top left`.** The panel is anchored `top: 100%; left: 0` relative to the badge that opened it (lines 17-18). A popover must appear to *come from* its trigger; scaling from `center` would make it grow away from the badge in all four directions and break that link. Top-left matches the anchor exactly.
docs/work/specs/DESIGN-SPEC-schedule-screen-20260807.md:616:| Switching badges | The rail unmounts and remounts; the new instance plays its own 180ms pop-in from its own anchor. This is correct — the panel genuinely moved to a new trigger. |

docs/work/specs/2026-08-26-roots-subscreens-redundancy-program.md:91:The critique's "converge two week-scoping models" is a **data-model decision**, not a control swap. Anchors store one `schedule_week_id` (all-weeks or exactly-one; `buildSchedule.js:111`, `AnchorsScreen.jsx:384`); Groups/Activities store an exclusion *set* (any subset; `GroupsScreen.jsx:17`). Converging silently narrows anchors or widens groups, and touches **both schedule routes**. Options to design: (a) leave the data models distinct but make the two controls *visually* consistent; (b) genuinely unify on the exclusion-set model with a migration. Requires a small before/after design + Red Hat pass before any code. Deferred out of the safe slice.

docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:34:Shoresh is a professional planning instrument for a camp director, not a consumer toy. The interface is grounded and quiet: paper-toned surfaces, thin hairline borders, generous whitespace, softly rounded corners, and shadows so faint they only separate a modal from the page behind it. Warmth lives in the paper background and the bronze accent — never in bounce, saturation, or decoration. It is precise: **color is a data channel, not ornament.** On the schedule grid — the product's visual center — colour is never decorative. It carries three independent channels: the activity ladder encodes how often an activity runs (frequency, a navy lightness ramp); anchor slate (`--anchor`) marks a slot that is locked/structural; and the flag colours (`FLAG_COLORS`) mark a flagged slot by severity. Each is deliberately desaturated and mutually separable rather than vivid.
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:45:Every current `:root` variable in `src/index.css` is covered, plus three new tokens (`--accent`, `--danger`, `--anchor`). "Meaning" states the semantic role — what the token is *for*.
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:59:| `--anchor` | *(new)* | `#5C6B7A` | **Muted slate.** Fixed / immovable / structurally-locked events on the grid. Reads as "locked structure," clearly *not* a colored activity, harmonizes with navy. Replaces `--purple`'s anchor role. |
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:60:| `--purple` | `#A63595` | **DEPRECATED** | Was the fixed/anchor-event color. Keep defined as an alias of `--anchor` until the retheme migrates callers (`SlotCell.ANCHOR_COLOR`). Do not use in new code. |
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:146:- **`--accent` (bronze `#B8833A`) carries the caution/attention role.** Anything meaning "temporary, needs attention, in progress, throttled" — the auth lockout box, a soft advisory — uses bronze, **not** red. This keeps red rare and therefore loud when it does appear (a core "quiet, precise" move: reserve the alarm color). There is intentionally **no separate amber `warning` token**; bronze is the caution hue. The shared primitive for this is `S.cautionBanner` (`src/styles/shared.js`) — `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 12%, var(--surface))` fill, `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 45%, var(--border))` hairline, `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 65%, var(--text))` text — used for advisory copy like "set this up first" (e.g. `GroupsScreen.jsx`, `AnchorsScreen.jsx`). Screens must route through it rather than hardcoding an amber block locally.
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:147:- **`--anchor` (slate `#5C6B7A`) vs the activity palette:** anchor is deliberately *outside* the six-rung activity ladder. Activity colour says "how often this activity runs" (frequency rung); anchor says "this slot is locked/structural and cannot be moved." Slate is low-chroma and cool, and is a distinct hue from the activity ladder's navy, so it never competes with or is mistaken for a frequency rung — it reads as chrome/lock, not data. The grid legend must document anchor **separately** from the activity key.
docs/governance/standards/DESIGN_STANDARD.md:208:- `src/components/schedule/SlotCell.jsx` — `ACTIVITY_COLORS` array and `FLAG_COLORS` (both now imported from `slotCellConstants.js`). _Prior: this bullet also listed `ANCHOR_COLOR = '#A63595'` (→ `--anchor`); that migration is done — `slotCellConstants.js` now defines `ANCHOR_COLOR = 'var(--anchor)'`._
